If the mic says voice isn't available, install/enable Google's speech services and download the English offline pack (Settings → General management → Voice input). On the Mac, "Add to MEKA" may need ticking once in System Settings → Keyboard → Keyboard Shortcuts → Services → Text.
- [x] Lists: Waiting for (with chase dates), Someday (with kinds), Decisions (with review dates)
  - *2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `Lists` (non-AI). Waiting for is a `commitment` owed to you: what, from whom, a chase date (presets Tomorrow · In 3 days · Next week · In 2 weeks · No date; default 3 days); "Chased" records it and sets the next chase 3 days on; "Got it" closes it (Done wins over a chase made at the same time on the other device). Someday is a task with lifecycle SOMEDAY grouped by kind (Ideas · To buy · Projects · Trips · To read · To look into · Applications · Home · Other), never in Today or the planner; "Do it now" puts it back in Today, and a one-off task's detail has "Someday" to send it there (repeating tasks can't). Decisions: what you decided, why, an optional review date (a month · 3 months · 6 months · a year); when due: "Still right" with the next review, Revisit, or Replace (the old one is kept as superseded, so it isn't re-made). Chase and review dates are days (stored as 09:00 local). Due chases and reviews show as one "From your lists" card in Needs you and count in its badge. Unit-tested incl. two-device sync. Fold: Lists tab with a springing tab pill, rows that unfold their actions, date chips and add fields. Mac: the same with a segmented control and menus.
  - *Later, if wanted:* pick "from whom" from contacts / People (V1); a received item asking to become a task; showing superseded decisions as history; "promises spotted in email → Waiting for" is V1.
- [x] Goals and habits (progress, streaks, planner makes room for habits that are behind)
  - *2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `Goals` (non-AI), unit-tested incl. two-device sync. Habits: how often (every day … once a week, Monday–Sunday weeks), when (morning · afternoon · evening · any time), how long; tick or untick today (a tick on the Fold and the Mac the same day is one tick). Pace: behind / to do today / on track / week done, pro rata in the week a habit is added; streaks count days for a daily habit and weeks that met the target otherwise. Goals: what done looks like, a horizon (weeks · months · years), progress counted from linked habits and tasks (or set by hand in 10 % steps while nothing is linked), Finished keeps it out of the list. Plan my day makes room first for habits that are behind or due, in their part of the day ("↻ Stretch · behind"); habit blocks are shown, not applied. Fold: Goals tab (tick circle pops with a spring and a light haptic, streak number rolls, week dots, progress bars fill on appear, rows unfold their settings) and a Goal row in the task detail. Mac: the same screen with menus, Goal menu in the task detail, habit blocks in the plan sheet.
  - *Later, if wanted:* ticking a habit for an earlier day from the screen (the core allows it); habits in Today; auto-ticks from Health Connect (V1).
- [x] Fasting tracker (timer, eating window, planner-aware, weekly history)
  - *2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `Fasting` (non-AI, no health claims), unit-tested incl. two-device sync. A fast is a `fast` entity (start, goal in hours, end); you start it now or up to 3 h back ("I started an hour ago"), end it, nudge the start (−1 h · −30 min · +30 min), change the goal (12–24 h), undo "End fast" for 10 minutes, or discard a mistaken one. Plan presets 14:10 · 16:8 · 18:6 · 20:4 set the usual goal and the eating window (16:8, eating 12:00–20:00 until changed); the card says "Eating window open until 20:00" / "opens at 12:00" / "closed at 20:00". Two devices starting offline: the earlier start counts, ending ends both, history merges overlaps. History: the last 7 days (each fast on the day it ended) and "5 fasts in 7 days · average 16 h 12 m · 4 reached the goal". Plan my day keeps meals free ("◐ Break your fast" at the goal when it lands today, "◐ Last meal before your fast" before the window closes); nothing is planned over them, and they are shown, not applied. Fold: a Fasting card at the top of Goals (ring sweeps continuously with a one-second timer, soft pulsing glow at the goal, light haptic on start/end, chips to start/adjust/choose a plan, 7 day bars fill on appear). Mac: the same section with menus (Start a fast clicks to start now), `TimelineView` timer, bars and meal rows in the plan sheet.
  - *Later, if wanted:* goal-reached / 16·18·24 h nudges on the Fold (windowed alarms per ADR-007) with the notification governor; a fasting line in Today; custom eating-window times (presets only for now); editing or deleting past fasts.
- [x] Evening shutdown (tick, carry over, tomorrow preview)
  - *2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `EveningShutdown` (non-AI), unit-tested incl. two-device sync. Offered from the end of work on a work day (when work ends 15:00–22:00), else 18:00, while you're not at work and until you shut down; shutting down on either device puts the card away on both until the next evening (stored as the `shutdown` context-mode entity, ADR-008). The screen: "4 done today"; left from today (what Today shows plus plans from earlier days never done; planned first, then overdue, then the rest), each with a tick, Tomorrow (the same snooze as the task detail: times of day kept), Skip (repeating) or Someday (one-off), and "Move the rest to tomorrow"; then Tomorrow · Wed 7 Oct: work hours, all-day events, events and planned tasks in time order (an event still running from tonight shows "Until 01:00"), tasks with no time, and "2 events · 4 tasks · first at 09:30". Fold: a "Shut down the day" card in Today in the evening ("3 left · tomorrow: 2 events, first at 09:30"), a Shut down link in Today's header any time, and the shutdown pane; after it, one quiet line ("Day shut down at 18:42 · Tomorrow: …") reopens it. Mac: the same card, header button and a sheet.
  - *Later, if wanted:* a shutdown nudge at the start of the evening (notification governor item); habits and fasting in the tomorrow preview; choosing the evening start time.
- [x] Renewals and bills radar, manual entry (Obligation entity: MOT, insurance, boiler, subscriptions, cancel-by dates)
  - *2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `Renewals` (non-AI) over the `obligation` entity, unit-tested incl. two-device sync. Kinds: MOT · Car tax · Insurance · Boiler service · Subscription · Bill · Licence or passport · Warranty · Other, each with a default lead time (MOT and boiler 4 weeks, insurance 3, car tax and warranty 2, licence/passport 2 months, subscriptions and bills 3 days; changeable) and a default repeat (yearly, monthly, or none). A due date (any day within ten years), repeat (doesn't repeat · every month · every 3 months · every year, anchored on the due date; the 31st stays the 31st after a short month), cost in pounds (typed, e.g. "9.99"; a bad one is refused with a message) and a cancel-by date (the day before … 30 days before). "Renewed" / "Paid" / "Serviced" / "MOT done" rolls a repeating one to its next date (the cancel-by date keeps its distance; doing it on both devices offline moves it on once, no conflict) and closes a one-off; "Cancelled it" / "Stop tracking" takes it off the radar (kept as cancelled). The radar: Needs doing (overdue, cancel-by within a week, inside the lead time), Coming up (about 3 months), Later; "renews Thu 12 Nov · cancel by Thu 5 Nov · £412 a year"; repeating costs add up to "£60.99 a month · £731.88 a year". What needs doing counts in the Lists line, the Needs you "From your lists" card and its badge ("1 renewal due · 1 to cancel or keep"). Fold: a fourth Lists tab, Renewals (counts beside tab labels only when there's room), rows unfold chips for repeat, lead time, cancel-by and kind, a cost field and a date button (the system date picker in MEKA's colours). Mac: the same tab with menus and a date field.
  - *Later, if wanted:* a reminder notification at the lead time (notification governor item); linking to the car/home/person it's for once People and the Vault land (free-text "for" is stored but not yet editable in the apps); other currencies (GBP only); renewals in the morning brief; letters and emails filling the radar (V1).
- [x] Notification governor v1: tiers, quiet hours, digest at 12:30 and 18:00 (local notifications)
  - *2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `Governor` (non-AI), unit-tested incl. two-device sync. Tiers (ADR-013): Critical posts at once even in quiet hours; Needs a decision posts at once or when quiet hours end; Heads-up posts at its time, but one that falls in quiet hours rides in the next digest instead (with digests off, it posts when quiet hours end) and a stale one is dropped; Digest is summed up at 12:30 and 18:00 ("Evening digest · 3 things", one line each, "1 renewal due · 2 to chase"); App only never posts. A digest missed by more than 2 h is skipped; a heads-up due at digest time rides in it; more than 3 heads-ups at once fold into one ("4 things need you"). `NoticeSources` derives notices on every evaluation, nothing stored: cancel-by dates (heads-up at 09:00 the day before), fasting goal reached (heads-up at the goal, stale after 3 h), "Shut down the day" (heads-up when the evening starts, not once shut down), and in the digest renewals and bills due, things to chase, decisions to review and overdue tasks. Settings sync (`context_mode/quiet`: quiet hours default 22:00–07:00, each digest on/off, any source lowered to Digest or App only, never raised); what this device posts (Everything · Digests only · Off) and what it has posted stay on the device. Fold: one channel per tier (Critical, Needs a decision, Heads-ups, Digests; titles off the lock screen), an inexact `setWindow` alarm for the next digest, the end of quiet hours or the next heads-up (ADR-007, no exact alarms), re-armed after a reboot or update; it looks again whenever lists, fasting, the shutdown, Today or the settings change; tapping opens the right tab (a digest opens Needs you or Lists). Notifications pane from Today's header (chips, quiet-hour steppers unfold, digest toggles, "what goes where" per source, preview lines "Quiet until 07:00 · Next digest 18:00 · 3 things so far"). Mac: the same sheet from Today; this Mac is Off until turned on there (then macOS asks once); posts with macOS notifications (digests passive), checked every 30 s.
  - *Check on the Fold:* open Today → Notifications and allow notifications; let the "Heads-ups" channel make a sound if you want one (Settings → Notifications → Meka). Each tier is its own channel, so Do Not Disturb can let "Critical" through.
  - *Later, if wanted:* the interruptions count for the weekly review (ADR-013); moving the work-mode alerts and the after-work nudge onto the governor's channels and quiet hours; choosing digest times (12:30 and 18:00 can be turned off, not moved); renewal "due today" heads-ups; Mac notification taps opening the right section.
- [x] Push notifications via Firebase — project `meka-os-5ae41` created 2026-10-07; `android/app/google-services.json` committed. Server sends through FCM HTTP v1 with the service account in Secrets Manager `meka-os-dev/fcm/service-account` (service account pasted by Meka 2026-10-07; empty `{}` = push off, keep local notifications as the fallback).
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-07 (Fold and server; the Mac has no push until Needs Meka #6, its long-poll keeps it current while open; core touched, so Mac checked nightly):* "sync now" wake-ups (ADR-004 amendment). Server: `Push` + `FcmSender` (backend/Push.kt, Fcm.kt): when a device stores ops, the household's other devices are sent one data-only FCM message `{"t":"sync"}` (normal priority, collapse key `sync`, TTL 1 h; nothing about Meka's data goes to Google), at most one every 20 s per device with one trailing wake at the end of the gap; sent off the request. The service account is read from Secrets Manager at use time (5-min cache, `{}` = off, refused unless it's a `service_account` for Google's own token endpoint); the RS256 JWT-bearer assertion is signed with the JDK, the access token cached; a token FCM calls unregistered is forgotten. `POST /v1/push/token` (signed, keyed devices only; empty token removes it) into `push_token` (V6 migration, additive; revoked devices never woken). Wire: `WireCodec.PushToken` (shape-checked). Core: `PushApi` on the HTTP transport, `MekaCore.registerPushToken` (false = try later, e.g. a server without push). Unit-tested: wire, transport, the coalescing, gone tokens, other households, the JWT (verified against the key), the FCM request and token reuse, the Postgres store. Fold: Firebase Messaging 24.1.0 (FCM only, no Analytics; analytics/ad-id collection meta-data off), configured from `res/values/firebase.xml` (the google-services plugin's values, checked against google-services.json by `PushTest`, no extra Gradle plugin); `push/MekaMessagingService` runs `SyncWorker.syncSoon` (expedited one-off, network-constrained) on a wake, so Today, the widgets, ongoing notifications and the governor follow a Mac edit within seconds; the token is sent once per new token (on start, after connecting, and retried at each background sync until the server has it); nothing is shown for a wake. CI green (run 37688171086; macOS job at the next nightly run; the core and server were touched, so the server redeploys and applies V6).
  - *Slice 2, 2026-10-07 (Mac checked nightly):* server-side changes wake the devices, and a moved kick-off is announced (ADR-007's server-originated case). Server: a calendar or fixtures poll that wrote ops calls `Push.serverChanged`, which wakes every device of the household (same 20 s coalescing; headline refreshes never wake), so a moved meeting's reminder re-arms and the governor sees changes within seconds of the 5-minute poll. When the fixtures feed moves a timed match whose old kick-off was still ahead, the server writes `movedFromMs`/`movedAtMs` on it (additive, fixtures only; ADR-008 addendum). Core: `FixtureMoves` (pure) and a new governor source, **Kick-off changes** (Heads-up, can be lowered to Digest or App only; listed in Notifications on both apps): "Kick-off moved: Barça v Real Madrid" · "Now 21:00 · was 18:30 · Sat 10 Oct" ("Now Sun 11 Oct 18:30 · was Sat 10 Oct 21:00" when the day changed), once per new time, stale after two days or at kick-off; hidden fixtures stay quiet; tapping opens Today. Unit-tested (FixtureMovesTest incl. the governor; FixturesTest: the move recorded and every poll that wrote wakes the household, a quiet poll doesn't; PushTest). Visible on the Fold and the Mac through their governors (no new UI); the wake itself is Fold-only until #6.
  - *Remaining:* approvals as visible pushes come with V1's approvals inbox; APNs for the Mac once #6 lands; "Moved from 18:30" in the event detail, if wanted. Item done (Meka's key is in, #2). CI green (run 37689863689; macOS job at the next nightly run; the core and server were touched, so the server redeploys).

If it doesn't, Play services must be allowed to run MEKA in the background (Settings → Apps → Meka → Battery → Unrestricted helps on Samsung).

  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `MorningBrief` (non-AI), unit-tested incl. two-device sync. Offered from the end of quiet hours (07:00 when quiet hours are off or end outside 05:00–10:00) until noon, until "Got it" on either device (the `context_mode/brief` entity, ADR-008). The brief: "Good morning, Meka · Tue 6 Oct · Work 09:00–17:30"; Today in time order (all-day events, then events and planned tasks, then open tasks with no time, overdue ones marked) with "3 events · 5 tasks · first at 09:30"; Waiting on (chases due today first, lit, up to 5, "+2 more in Lists"); On your lists (renewals, bills and cancel-by dates that need doing, decisions due a review); You (habit pace, a running fast). A "Morning brief" heads-up goes out when it starts (new governor source, can be lowered to Digest or App only; stale at noon; taps open Today). Fold: card in Today in the morning, a Brief link in Today's header any time, and the brief pane. Mac: the same card, header button and a sheet.
  - *Slice 2, 2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* news headlines. Backend: `NewsProvider` (ADR-009) with a first adapter, `BbcNewsRss` — BBC News public RSS feeds for Top stories · UK · World · Politics · Business · Technology · Science · Health · Football (no sign-in, no key, nothing about Meka sent; a plain GET from the server). Every household follows it like the fixtures feed (a "Headlines" row on the Calendars screen shows its status). At most hourly it mirrors the newest 4 items of each topic into fixed slots (`headline` entities, one per topic and slot, so the count never grows); a story still in the feed keeps its slot and isn't rewritten, a new one takes a freed slot, leftovers are marked removed. The XML parser refuses DTDs/external entities; only https links are kept, tracking parameters dropped. Core: `News` (non-AI), unit-tested incl. two-device sync: topics chosen in the brief (synced, `context_mode/news`; default Top stories and World; none is allowed), up to 5 headlines from the last 36 h, newest first, the same story in two topics shown once, "BBC News · World · 2 h ago". Headlines are untrusted (ADR-006): shown as plain text only, a link opens in the browser only when it is https. Fold: a Headlines section at the end of the brief, Topics unfolds chips; tapping a headline opens it. Mac: the same section with a Topics menu; clicking opens the browser.
  - *Later, if wanted:* headlines in the morning heads-up text; more sources (e.g. the Guardian) behind the same interface; the full news hub is V2.

Anthropic publishes no feed, so it isn't included; BBC Sport's Barcelona page was left out (unverified). The parser now reads Atom as well as RSS, keeps each item's publisher (Google News's " - Marca" title suffix moves into the source), keeps the feed's summary as plain text (≤ 400 chars; none from Google News or Hacker News, whose descriptions are links/points), and keeps meaningful query parameters while dropping tracking ones (utm_*, at_*, oc, click ids). Feeds are **verified at every refresh**: a feed that fails or has nothing usable is skipped and the topic fails (the row says "Couldn't sync") only when none of its feeds could be read.   - *Images, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly):* pictures on every story. **Server** (backend/integrations/NewsImages.kt): the parser reads each item's picture (`media:thumbnail` / image `media:content`, the one nearest 320 px wide; an image `enclosure`; Atom `rel="enclosure"`; else the first `<img>` in the description or content), https only. At each hourly refresh `NewsImages` fetches pictures it doesn't have yet (public hosts only, checked on every redirect; image types only; ≤ 5 MB; at most 40 new and 90 s per refresh; WebP isn't asked for), decodes them with the JDK's ImageIO (size checked first; logos and pixels under 64 px refused) and re-encodes a **JPEG ≤ 320 × 320 px and ≤ 30 KB** (JPEG instead of WebP: the JDK can't write WebP and a native encoder isn't worth it at this size). Kept in the server's **own database** (migration V7 `news_image`, additive; shared by every household, deleted 3 days after no feed mentions it; a few MB, so no S3 bucket and no cost). Headlines carry only the server's key (`headline.image`, ADR-008 addendum), never the publisher's address; devices fetch `POST /v1/news/image` (keyed devices only; publisher refused). **Core:** `Headline/NewsItem.imageKey` (anything not a 32-hex key ignored), `NewsItem.tileInitial`, `MekaCore.newsImage` / `newsImageBase64` (Mac) with an in-memory cache (100 pictures, misses not re-asked for 10 min, nothing on disk). **Fold:** each story in News shows its picture on the right (72 × 54, cropped), the detail leads with it (16:9); a tile with the source's initial (Barça's colour on Barça stories) stands in until it arrives, and the picture cross-fades in. **Mac:** the same in the News sheet. - [x] Weekly review with north-star metrics (ADR-013)
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `WeeklyReview` (non-AI), unit-tested incl. two-device sync. One Monday–Sunday week: tiles (Done · Habit ticks "of 10" · Fasts · Lists closed) and "3 more done than the week before"; Done (tasks finished in the week, newest first, up to 12 and "and 3 more", with the day); Habits (ticks against the week's target, pro rata in the week one was added, dots for the days, "5 of 7 · met"; habits added after the week left out); Goals (progress now, "2 tasks done this week"); Fasting ("4 fasts · average 16 h 10 m · 3 reached the goal") and lists ("2 things you were waiting for arrived", "1 decision made", "1 renewal or bill dealt with"); Still open (planned or due in the week and not done, lit); the week ahead ("Next week", or "This week" when looking back at last week: events, tasks planned or due, renewals and bills due, decisions to review, chases); and the six north-star metrics, each saying when it starts counting (none can be counted before MEKA acts, reminds or asks for you). Opens on last week Monday–Wednesday, this week from Thursday; ‹ › step back up to 12 weeks (a screen choice, not synced). "Done reviewing" syncs (`context_mode/review`, ADR-008). Fold: the Review tab (was a placeholder). Mac: the Review sidebar section, ⌘[ ⌘] step weeks.
  - *Slice 2, 2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* the Sunday-evening nudge. Core: `ReviewCard` on the review view (the week ending on Sunday, or just ended on Monday; offered from 18:00 Sunday through Monday until that week is reviewed on either device; "Review your week" / "Review last week" with "12 done · 3 of 4 habits met · 2 still open"), unit-tested incl. two-device sync; a new governor source, Weekly review (Heads-up at 18:00 on Sunday, one key per week so it posts once, stale when Monday ends, can be lowered to Digest or App only; at 18:00 it usually rides in the evening digest); a new notice target, Review. Fold: the card rises into Today; tapping it (or the notification) opens the Review tab on that week. Mac: the same card in Today, opening the Review section on that week.
  - *Slice 3, 2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* Interruptions counted. Core: `Interruptions` (non-AI), unit-tested incl. two-device sync: after each governor run the platform reports what actually reached you (`notificationsPosted`); only Critical, Needs a decision and Heads-up count (digests never do); counts only, one `interruption_day` entity per device per day (ADR-008), never text. Reporting even with nothing posted starts the count (`countingSince`), so earlier weeks say "Counted from Tue 6 Oct" rather than 0. The review's Interruptions line: "7" with "5 heads-ups · 2 needed a decision · 3 fewer than the week before" (compared only when the week before was counted in full; "None reached you" for 0). Fold: counts posts to channels that aren't turned off in the phone's settings. Mac: counts what macOS accepted while Meka is allowed to notify (nothing while this Mac is Off). Both apps: a numeric north-star value counts up on appear.
  - *Later, if wanted:* count the Fold's work-mode alerts and after-work nudge once they move onto the governor's channels; the other north-star metrics arrive with the activity log, approvals and the AI layer (V1).
- [x] Search everything (tasks, events, lists, decisions; documents when the Vault lands)
  - *2026-10-06 (Mac checked nightly):* core `Search` (non-AI, local), unit-tested incl. two-device sync. Searches what is already synced on the device; nothing is sent anywhere, stored or indexed (the data is small enough to scan per keystroke). Every word typed must start a word in the item ("den" finds "Dentist", "boil serv" finds "Boiler service"); case, common accents and apostrophes ignored; at least 2 letters. Titles weigh more than places and people, which weigh more than notes; a title holding the whole phrase comes first. Searched: open tasks (title, notes, steps), calendar events (title, place, calendar; upcoming soonest first, then past), Waiting for (what, from whom, notes), decisions incl. replaced ones (statement, why), renewals and bills (title, what it's for, kind, notes), Someday, goals (title, what done looks like), habits, and done tasks (a routine's done occurrences fold into the latest). Groups in a fixed order (Tasks · Calendar · Waiting for · Decisions · Renewals and bills · Someday · Goals · Habits · Done), 20 per group with "and 5 more · keep typing to narrow"; each result has its line ("Planned today 14:00 · ↻ Every weekday", "Tomorrow · 20:00–22:00 · Camp Nou", "Done yesterday", the list row's own line) and a snippet when the match is in the notes, steps or rationale. Results follow edits and sync while the query is set (`MekaCore.search` / `searchView`). Fold: "Search" first in Today's header opens the search pane: an open task opens its detail over the results, a done one unfolds Reopen, a list item opens Lists on its tab with the row unfolded, a goal or habit opens Goals with the row unfolded, events are shown only. Mac: ⌘F (Edit → Search Everything…) or "Search" in Today opens a sheet with the detail beside the results; the same rules.
  - *Later, if wanted:* documents when the Vault lands; people (V1); searching headlines and held work messages (kept out for now: headlines are untrusted and the held messages are Fold-only); highlighting the matched words.

- [x] Self-updating phone app (checks the server for a newer signed APK; you tap Install once)
  - *2026-10-07 (Mac checked nightly):* the Mac publishes, the Fold offers, Meka taps Install. `tools/publish-fold.sh` (on the Mac that installs MEKA, so the debug signing key matches) builds the APK and opens `mekaos://publish-fold-update?apk=…`; MEKA on the Mac (or File → Publish Fold Update… and pick the APK) shows "MEKA 0.1.412 · build 412 · 24.3 MB", checks it's a MEKA APK with Gradle's `output-metadata.json` beside it, and on Publish uploads it in 1 MiB chunks over the normal signed requests (each chunk retried; what arrived is kept). Server: `/v1/releases/{latest,chunk,upload}` for keyed devices only, per household; a build must be newer than the published one (the same build again is a no-op, the same number with other contents is refused); offered only once every chunk is in and the whole file matched its SHA-256; the newest two builds are kept (V5 migration, additive: `app_release`, `app_release_chunk`; tens of MB in Postgres, no new AWS resources). Every build's versionCode is now the commit count (`-Pmeka.versionCode` overrides), versionName `0.1.N`. Fold: checks on open (at most every 10 min) and with background sync; Today shows "MEKA update ready · Build 412 · 24.3 MB" with Install and Later (Later hides that build only). Install asks once for Android's "Install unknown apps" switch, downloads straight into an Android install session with a filling bar, checks the SHA-256, then Android's own Install prompt (it refuses a build not signed like the installed app); if MEKA was in the background the prompt comes back when it opens. Core: `AppUpdateRules` (domain, pure), `ReleaseTransfer` + `MekaCore.latestRelease/downloadRelease/publishRelease` (facade), wire documents; unit-tested on the server (in memory and Postgres), the facade and the wire. CI green (run 70); server deployed (deploy run 70).
  - *Check on the Fold:* install this build once more the old way (`tools/install-fold.sh`); from then on run `tools/publish-fold.sh` on the Mac and tap Install on the Fold. The first time, Android asks to allow MEKA to install apps (Settings opens; turn it on, come back, tap Install). Android's developer verification (ADR-010) will cover these installs when it reaches the UK; the free Limited Distribution account keeps them working.
  - *Later, if wanted:* a quiet "update ready" notification when MEKA isn't open; showing the installed build somewhere in settings; release notes from the commit messages.
- [x] Export and backup (one-tap export of all data as JSON + documents)
  - *2026-10-07 (Mac checked nightly):* core `DataExport` (domain, non-AI, pure), unit-tested incl. two-device sync, and `DataExportCodec` + `MekaCore.exportAll/exportSummary` (facade), unit-tested. The export is this device's replica as it stands: every entity type (tasks, steps, waiting for, decisions, renewals and bills, goals, habits and their ticks, fasts and the fasting plan, calendar events, settings such as quiet hours, work hours and the review/brief/shutdown marks, interruption counts, household and people), the winning value of each field and, where two devices disagree and nobody has chosen yet, every value on offer (`conflicts`), so nothing is lost. Deleted entities and BBC headlines (not Meka's data) are left out. One readable JSON file, `meka-export-2026-10-07.json` (`format: meka-os-export`, `version: 1`): values keep their type, every `…AtMs` time has a readable UTC copy beside it (`…At`), stable order so the same data exports byte for byte the same, `documents: []` until the Vault lands. "312 items" / "214 tasks · 48 calendar events · 12 renewals and bills · 9 settings". Nothing is sent anywhere; the file isn't encrypted and the screen says so. Fold: "Your data" in Today's header opens the pane, and the Vault screen (open Fold) holds the same section; Export everything builds the file, Android's own save screen picks where (Downloads, Drive, …), MEKA writes only there. Mac: "Your data" in Today, File → Export All Data… (⇧⌘E) and the Vault section; the save panel picks where. CI green (run 71; macOS job at the next nightly run).
  - *Check on the Fold:* Today → Your data → Export everything, save to Downloads, and open the file in a text viewer.
  - *Later, if wanted:* restoring from an export (import); a scheduled backup (e.g. weekly into a folder Meka picks); a password-protected export; exporting the server's full op log (history, not just how things stand now); Vault documents in the export (V2).

Core: `EventActions` (non-AI), unit-tested incl. two-device sync, over a new `event_mark` entity (`hidden`, LWW) and a new task field `eventId` (both additive; ADR-008 addendum). A prep task is "Prepare for Call with Tunde", due at the event's start, planned 30 min before it for 15 min (unplanned if that time has gone; an all-day event's is due 09:00 on its day); its id comes from the event (`p<eventId>`), so a double tap or both devices offline make one task, and adding it after deleting or finishing it brings it back. Hidden events leave Today's timeline, Plan my day, the brief, the shutdown and the review (Search still finds them); the Calendar tab lists them at the foot of their day ("1 hidden from your day") with Show, and a day holding only hidden events still shows. `MekaCore.eventMarks`, `addPrepTask`, `hideEvent`, `showEvent`; the event detail says "Prep task at 13:30" / "Prep task done" / "Hidden from your day". Fold: swipe an upcoming event right (accent, "Prep task") or left ("Hide from my day") in Today's timeline or the Calendar tab: the label pops with a tick haptic at the threshold, letting go acts with a light haptic and the row springs back; an undo bar rises for 5 s (Undo deletes the prep task / shows the event); screen readers get both as custom actions; the detail pane has Prep task and Hide/Show buttons. Mac: the same in each event row's context menu and as hover buttons (the row lifts 2 pt), the undo bar rises over the screen, and the detail sheet has the buttons. Core: `ReminderRules` (non-AI, pure) and `EventActions.setReminder/setLeaveBy`, unit-tested incl. two-device sync and through the governor, over two new `event_mark` fields, `remindMin` and `travelMin` (additive, LWW; ADR-008 addendum). Remind me 5 · 10 · 15 · 30 min before a timed event ("Call with Tunde" · "In 10 min · 14:00 · Room 4"); Leave by, offered when the event has a place, asks how long it takes to get there (10 · 15 · 20 · 30 · 45 min · 1 h; Meka's own estimate, no maps service) and says "Leave now for Dentist" · "Starts 14:00 · 30 min away · High St Surgery" at start − travel. Only times still ahead are offered. Both go through the notification governor as a new source, Event reminders (Heads-up, can be lowered to Digest or App only; quiet hours and each device's setting apply; tapping opens Today); a reminder that can't post before the event starts is dropped, never sent late; a moved event reminds at its new time; hidden, all-day and started events don't remind; what reached Meka shows in Activity like any reminder. `MekaCore.setEventReminder/setEventLeaveBy`; the detail says "Reminder 10 min before · Leave by 13:30 · 30 min away". The event detail has Remind me and Leave by chip rows (the set chip fills with a colour blend; tap it again to turn it off). - [x] Fasting v2 (Meka, 2026-10-07: "some weeks I fast for 5 days; just track, remind me and keep the history"): two modes — **daily window** (as built) and **extended fast** (start "5-day fast" or "until Friday 18:00"); a running fast shows days + hours ("Day 3 of 5 · 62 h") on Today and as an ongoing notification; gentle check-ins (once a day, quiet hours respected) and a "you did it" moment with a ring burst at the end; ending early is fine and recorded as is. **History**: every fast with start, end, planned vs actual, streak of completed fasts, a calendar heat strip; export with the rest. Start/stop from Ask ("start a 5-day fast"), the card or the notification. Tracking only: no food, calorie or weight advice.
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-07 (Mac checked nightly):* extended fasts and the history. Core: `Fasting` gains the extended kind (`startExtended(hours)`: 24 h · 36 h · 2 · 3 · 5 · 7 days; `startUntil(untilMs)`: "Until Tue 18:00" … six days ahead, at least 12 h away; goals up to ten days) over two new `fast` fields, `kind` and `goalAtMs` (additive, LWW; ADR-008 addendum). A running extended fast is "5-day fast" / "Fast until Fri 18:00" with "Day 3 of 5 · 62 h" (past the goal "Day 6 · 122 h · goal reached") and "Goal 5 days · Sat 20:00"; a nudge of its start isn't refused for being more than two days back once the fast already is; the planner plans no meals around it until its goal day. `FastingHistory` (derived, nothing stored): every finished fast newest first (30 shown; all kept and exported) with planned against actual ("2 d 02 h of 3 days · ended early" · "16 h 20 m · goal reached") and when ("Mon 5 Oct 20:00 – Tue 6 Oct 12:00"); the streak ("3 fasts in a row reached the goal"); totals ("42 fasts · 31 reached the goal · longest 4 d 18 h"); a twelve-week Monday–Sunday heat strip of hours fasted each day (a running fast counts to now; levels under 8 h · under 16 h · under a day · the whole day). The ongoing notification and the Mac menu bar say "5-day fast · Day 3 of 5" (looked at again when the day turns), the Fast widget "5-day fast" with "Goal at Sat 20:00", the brief "5-day fast · Day 3 of 5 · 62 h"; the weekly review counts an "until" fast against its own goal. Unit-tested incl. two-device sync (domain, ongoing, facade). `MekaCore.startExtendedFast/startFastUntil`. - [~] Gym (Meka, 2026-10-07: getting back into it): MEKA's job is getting you there, not programming workouts. A "Gym" habit with a target (e.g. 3×/week) and preferred times; the planner **books the slots** around the calendar (shown in Next up, leave-by reminder); after the slot one tap asks "Went?" (or it ticks itself from a Galaxy Watch workout via Health Connect when that's on); an optional one-line note ("push day", "5 km") and an optional rotating label (Push · Pull · Legs) so you know what's next. Missed sessions are rebooked, not nagged. Detailed workout programming stays in a fitness app; MEKA links to it.
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly):* the booking, "Went?" and the rotation. **Core** (core/domain/Sessions.kt, non-AI, pure): `SessionRules.book` books a habit with **Book my sessions** on: from today to Sunday, as many sessions as the week still needs (its target pro rata, less the days ticked), each in the habit's part of the day when there's room (evening 17:00–21:30, morning 06:30–12:00, afternoon 12:00–17:00), else any time 07:00–21:30, clear of timed events (15 min either side to get there and back, an hour before kick-off) and of work hours (15 min after work; bank holidays and weekends free; a night shift keeps its morning), with a rest day between sessions while the week leaves room, and two booked habits never overlapping. Worked out afresh from what's there, so a meeting on the slot moves it and **"Didn't go" or a slot that passed unticked rebooks the remaining sessions** on the days left (never a nag); today's slot is worked out from the whole day, so it stays put and becomes "Did you go?" once over. The **rotation** (No rotation · Push · Pull · Legs · Upper · Lower · Full body A · B) labels sessions in turn from the last one ticked. Today's card: "Today 17:45–18:45" · "Now · until 18:45" · "Did you go? · 17:45–18:45" (Went · Didn't go) · "Went · 2 of 3 this week" with the note and "Next: Thu 17:45 · Pull" · "Not today · no worries" with "Rebooked for Tue 17:45" or "No other slot this week"; Undo. Goals' line "Booked Today 17:45 · Wed 17:45 · Fri 17:45" / "Week done · 3 of 3" / "· no room for 1 more". The planner keeps today's session free and shows it (`DayPlanner.plan(sessions)`); booked habits aren't placed again as flexible ones. Five additive fields (ADR-008 addendum): `habit.bookSlots`, `habit.rotation`, `habit_completion.missed/label/note`. `MekaCore.sessionsView`, `addGym`, `setHabitBooked`, `setHabitRotation`, `sessionWent`, `sessionMissed`, `setSessionNote`, `undoSession`; habits may now be 90 min. Tests: SessionsTest (after work, a meeting and a fixture, bank holidays, ticked days and a met week, did you go, rebooking, a short week, rotation, notes, a habit added late, two habits, night shifts, two devices, the planner), MekaCoreTest (the whole day across two cores), Mac bridge test. **Fold:** Goals → "Add Gym" (3 a week, evenings, an hour, booked) until a booked habit exists; a habit's settings gain "Book my sessions" On/Off and Rotation chips, and its row the booked line in the accent colour; Today shows the session card under Up next (goals/SessionCard.kt; motion catalogue row added). **Mac:** the same: Add Gym, a "Book my sessions" switch and Rotation menu, the line, and the card on Today (Goals/SessionCards.swift) with a note field after Went. Mac: alarms as critical notifications. Motion: the dismiss slider glows, the brief rises in.
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly):* the **smart wake alarm**. **Core** (core/domain/Alarms.kt, non-AI, pure, unit-tested): `AlarmRules.firstCommitment` (the first timed event from 04:00 on the wake day, or the start of work on a work day, bank holidays and a manual Work off excluded; events win a tie) and `suggest` (less the get-ready buffer, rounded down to five minutes, never before 04:00; nothing early → no suggestion, "Nothing early tomorrow · set one if you like", 07:00 offered); the wake day is tomorrow, or today before 04:00. `WakeView`: "Tomorrow · Fri 9 Oct", the time, "Suggested from Standup at 08:00 · 1 h to get ready" → "Alarm set · Standup at 08:00, 1 h to get ready"; a set alarm stays put, and when the suggestion moves earlier (a new 07:30 call) it says "Alarm set · Early call at 07:30 now, so 06:30 gives you 1 h" with **Use 06:30**. A new synced `alarm` entity (ADR-008 addendum, additive, LWW; id `wake.d<day>`) and `context_mode/alarms.wakeBufferMin` (30 · 45 · 60 · 75 · 90 · 120 min offered; 15 min–3 h accepted). Snooze 9 min ("Snoozed until 06:54"); Dismiss on either device ends it on both; unanswered it rings 10 minutes and is never rung late. `MekaCore.wakeView / nextAlarm / setWake / useSuggestedWake / wakeOff / setWakeBuffer / snoozeAlarm / dismissAlarm / ringingAlarm`. Tests: AlarmsTest (suggestions, bank holiday, the evening view, after midnight, ring/snooze/dismiss across two devices, ten minutes, set again), MekaCoreTest (BST, two cores), Mac bridge test. **Fold:** the shutdown pane gains **Wake alarm** after Tomorrow (‹ 07:45 ›, Set alarm / Turn off, Use 06:30, "Time to get ready" chips; "May ring up to 5 min late · Allow on time" until Alarms & reminders is allowed). `AlarmScheduler` registers the next alarm with `setAlarmClock` (ADR-007 addendum; else a 5-minute window), re-armed at process start; `AlarmRingService` (media-playback foreground service) plays the phone's alarm sound on the alarm stream, rising from 5 % to full over 30 s (`AlarmRouting.volumeAt`), vibrates, stops when answered on either device or after 10 min; a full-screen notification (Snooze 9 min · Dismiss) opens `AlarmActivity` over the lock screen: the time large, what it's for, Snooze, and **Slide to dismiss** with a glowing, breathing brass thumb; Dismiss asks to unlock and springs the **morning brief** up over Today. AlarmRoutingTest. **Mac:** the shutdown sheet's `WakeSection` (the same controls, the buffer as a menu); `MacAlarm` schedules the next alarm as a notification with Snooze 9 min · Dismiss (they sync; Dismiss opens the brief sheet). A critical/time-sensitive notification needs the Developer Program (#6, skipped), so it's an ordinary one with the default sound. - [~] Edit your calendars from MEKA (Meka, 2026-10-07: "one shop for my life") — right after Calendar actions. Add, edit, move and delete real Google (and later Outlook) events from both apps.
  - **Permission:** per account, switched on in Calendars ("Allow editing"); reconnecting asks Google for `calendar.events` (Microsoft: `Calendars.ReadWrite`). Read-only accounts keep working. Needs Meka #12 when it lands.
  - **You act, it happens:** your own taps apply straight away (no approval), with a 5-second Undo before the change is sent to Google. Edits made offline queue and send on reconnect; if the event changed in Google meanwhile, show both versions and let Meka choose (never overwrite silently).
  - **Guards:** deleting an event you organise with other guests says "This cancels it for 4 people" and needs a second tap; declining an invite (it notifies the organiser) is always an explicit tap, never automatic; MEKA itself never edits, declines or deletes others' events. Anything MEKA proposes from email or AI (later) goes through Needs you for approval first. Every change is in the activity log.
  - **Add event:** a sheet with title, day, time (or all day), calendar (default remembered), place, notes; natural typing ("Dentist Fri 3pm") fills the fields. Plan my day can also put its blocks in your calendar when you tap Apply (setting, off by default).
  - **Motion:** the sheet springs up, a moved event slides to its new slot in the timeline, a deleted one collapses with the undo bar rising.
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly):* **the permission**. Nothing is written to a calendar yet. **Server:** `CalendarProvider.writeScope` (Google `calendar.events`, asked for beside `calendar.readonly`, which still lists the calendars; Microsoft `Calendars.ReadWrite`) is asked for only when the sign-in starts with editing (`POST /v1/integrations/{provider}/connect` now takes a signed `{"editing": true}`; an empty body stays read-only); the callback records `can_edit` only when editing was asked for and the granted scope holds the write scope word for word (`grants`: `calendar.events.readonly` doesn't count), so unticking it leaves the account connected read-only and the browser page says so; a read-only reconnect gives editing up; Microsoft redeems the code and refreshes with the same scopes. `POST /v1/integrations/{provider}/editing` (signed, keyed devices only) takes `{"email", "editing": false}` and stops editing at once (asking for `true` there is refused: allowing needs the provider's consent). Migration V8 (additive): `oauth_state.editing`, `integration_account.can_edit` (existing accounts read-only). The privacy page says MEKA changes a calendar only where editing was allowed. **Core:** `WireCodec.encodeConnectRequest/EditingChange`, `IntegrationAccount.canEdit` ("edit", absent = false); `ConnectedAccount.canEdit/editingLine/editingAction`; `MekaCore.startConnect(provider, editing)` and `stopCalendarEditing`; `CalendarAccessRules` (core/domain/CalendarAccess.kt, non-AI, pure: which accounts offer it — never the feeds —, the lines "Read-only · MEKA only reads this calendar" / "Editing allowed · events you add or change in MEKA go to Google", Allow editing · Stop editing, the header, the note before the browser opens, Reconnect keeping what it had). **Fold:** Calendars shows the editing line under each account (cross-fades) and an **Allow editing** / **Stop editing** action (tick haptic); Allow editing says what Google will ask and opens its page; Reconnect asks for editing again when the account had it. **Mac:** the same in the Calendars sheet (press style, the line cross-fades). Tests: CalendarAccessTest, WireCodecTest, HttpSyncTransportTest, backend IntegrationsTest (asked/unticked/look-alike/granted, refresh asks for it, read-only reconnect, stop editing incl. other accounts and households, the HTTP routes, the real providers' URLs, the Postgres store), Mac bridge test. CI green (run 37771587474; macOS job at the next nightly run; the core and server were touched, so the server redeploys with migration V8 and GitHub publishes the phone build).
  - *Slice 2a, 2026-10-08 (core touched, so Mac checked nightly):* **the write path** (server and core; no app screen yet, so nothing is sent until a device makes an edit). **Core** (core/domain/CalendarEdits.kt, non-AI, pure): a new synced `event_edit` entity (ADR-008 addendum, additive, LWW): `CalendarEdits.add/change/move/delete/undo` (only Google/Outlook accounts with editing allowed, never fixtures; a change writes only what it changes — title, time, place, notes — plus the event as MEKA saw it; Outlook notes only when adding, as MEKA mirrors only their preview); 5 s undo window (`sendAfterMs`); refusals in words ("Give it a title", "It has to end after it starts", "That's longer than a month", "Editing isn't allowed for … · Allow editing in Calendars", …); `CalendarEditRules.clashes` (only what the edit would overwrite: a move doesn't clash with a renamed event, a rename does; a delete clashes when the title or time moved on), `merged`, `line` ("Adding “Dentist” to Google" · "Moved “Dentist” in Google" · "“Dentist” changed in Google meanwhile · choose a version" · "This cancels it for 4 people" · "Couldn't send to Google · Reconnect Google in Calendars" · "Undone"), `open()` (what still needs Meka). **Server** (backend/integrations/CalendarWriter.kt): sends each edit once its window and a 2 s grace are over, under the account's lock, writing the outcome once (fixed op ids) and waking the devices; poked by a push that carries an edit, else every minute. Before sending it reads the provider's copy (`CalendarProvider.event`): gone → a delete is done, a change refused; someone else organises it → refused ("MEKA only changes your own events"); changed in what the edit touches → CLASH with their version; a delete with guests needs `guestsOk` (Meka's second tap) and then tells them. Adds are idempotent (Google: our own event id `meka<key>`; Outlook: `transactionId`); changes are PATCHes of only the changed fields. 401 → "Reconnect", 403 → refused, 5xx/timeouts retried 30 s → 30 min apart and given up after a day; the account is re-synced right after so every device shows the change. `event_mirror.remote_id` (migration V9, additive; filled by the next poll, an edit waits for it). Google and Graph adapters gained `event/createEvent/updateEvent/deleteEvent`; `PostgresOpStore.latestFields`. Tests: CalendarEditsTest (core), CalendarWriterTest (send once after the window, undo, move without clash, clash, others' events, guests, stopped editing, retries and giving up, reconnect/refused, waiting for the id, already gone, the request bodies), IntegrationsTest on Postgres (remote ids, reading edits back). CI green (run 37775086414; macOS job at the next nightly run; the core and server were touched, so the server redeploys with migration V9 and GitHub publishes the phone build).
  - *Slice 2b, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly):* **Add event**. **Core:** `AddEventRules` / `AddEventForm` / `AddEventView` (core/domain/AddEvent.kt, non-AI, pure): a fresh form on the lit day (today when earlier) at the next quarter hour at least 15 min away (09:00 on another day), an hour long, on the account Meka last added to while it can still edit (worked out from the synced `event_edit`s, so both devices remember it with no new field), else the first; steps on the form itself (`withDay` today…two years, `withAllDay`, `stepTime` a quarter hour within the day, `withLength` 30 min · 1 h · 1 h 30 · 2 h, `withAccount`, title/place/notes cut to the edit's limits); the draft (a timed event at the local time, an all-day one UTC midnight to midnight like the mirror); the view: Today · Tomorrow (+ the picked day) chips, "15:30" "until 16:30" (or "00:45 next day"), the summary "Tomorrow · 09:00–10:00" / "Sat 10 Oct · all day", the accounts, "Add to Google", the problem in words ("Allow editing on an account in Calendars first", "Choose a calendar", the edit's own refusals; nothing while only the title is missing) and, for Outlook, "Outlook keeps these notes; MEKA can't change them later". `EditLineRules.lines`: edits waiting or sending, those needing Meka (clash, refused, failed) from the last day, and those done in the last two minutes, newest first, at most three; undone ones say nothing. **Facade:** `MekaCore.calendarEditAccounts` (from the server's account list: `connectedAccounts`, `stopCalendarEditing` and the new `refreshCalendarAccounts`; offline keeps what was known), `calendarEditLines`, `addEventForm(day)`, `addEventView`, `addEvent` (Made → the edit's id, or Refused in words), `undoEventEdit`, `eventEditLine`; the device's `CalendarEdits` now checks `canEdit` against that list. Tests: AddEventTest (defaults, days and limits, the remembered account, no account, timed and all-day drafts in BST, steps and midnight, lengths, the lines, the sheet to an edit on both devices with Undo and the server's answer), MekaCoreTest (`addEventWaitsForAnAccountThatCanEditThenBecomesAnEditBothDevicesSee`), Mac bridge test. **Fold:** "+ Add event" beside the Calendar title (only while an account allows editing; Calendar reads the accounts when it appears) opens `AddEventPane` (calendar/AddEventPane.kt) over the agenda on the lit day: the title (focused), its summary line, When chips (Today · Tomorrow · Pick a date · All day), ‹ 15:30 › "until 16:30" and the length chips, Calendar chips when there are several, Place, Notes, the problem in red and the Add pill (dim until it can go); Add closes the pane and raises the undo bar with the edit's line and Undo (five seconds, `undoEventEdit`); the lines (`EditLines`) sit under the summary. **Mac:** an "Add event" button in the Calendar header opens `AddEventSheet` (Calendar/AddEventSheet.swift; ⏎ adds, Esc cancels; the calendar is a menu when there are several; Pick a date… is the graphical popover), the shell's undo bar with a new `.eventEdit` undo, and `EditLinesView` under the summary. The motion catalogue gains "Add event". No new fields, no migration. CI green (run 37777788433; macOS job at the next nightly run; the core was touched, so the server redeploys and GitHub publishes the phone build).
  - *Slice 2c-i, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly):* **change, move and delete from the event detail**. **Core** (core/domain/EditEvent.kt, non-AI, pure): `EditEventRules.start` fills the Add event form from the event (its own account, the time as it is, even off the quarter hour; a multi-day all-day event by its first day); `draft` keeps the event's own value word for word for every field Meka didn't touch (so a rename never rewrites long notes or trims a title) and the event's exact times unless the day, start, length or all day moved (a multi-day all-day event keeps its span); `view` reuses `AddEventView` with "Save to Google", Save only once something changed, the one account, and three new view fields (defaults keep Add as it was): `timeEditable` (false for an event that has started, ended or runs over a day: the When rows are left out with "Its time can't be changed from MEKA once it's started…"), `notesEditable` (Google only and notes within MEKA's 2,000; else a quiet line) and `deleteLabel` ("Delete from Google"); renaming an event over a year old is allowed (only a new time is held to the past limit). `needsGuestsOk` / `note`: the detail's line for the event's own latest edit (`EventEditNote`: "Moving “Dentist” in Google", the refusal lit, `waiting`), and when the server held a delete back with "This cancels it for 4 people" (its guests, Meka not yet confirmed) **Delete anyway**, Meka's second tap (`guestsOk`); Calendar's line then reads "“Standup” · This cancels it for 4 people · open it to delete anyway"; a refusal for someone else's event never offers it. `CalendarEditRules.changes` now compares fields as they'd be written (trimmed, cut to MEKA's limits). `EventDetailView.editable/edit`. **Facade:** `canEditEvent`, `editEventForm`, `editEventView`, `saveEventEdit` (only what changed; a new time alone is a move), `deleteEvent(event, guestsOk)`, `deletingLine`; the detail reads the synced edits as last refreshed.   - *Slice 2c-ii, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly):* **a change shows before Google answers**. **Core** (core/domain/PendingEdits.kt, non-AI, pure): `PendingEditRules.apply` lays Meka's own edits over the mirrored events, oldest first: while an edit waits or is on its way (offline too) an add is a provisional event (`pending.<edit id>`, on the account's main calendar when the mirror names it: Google the address, Outlook "Calendar"), a change moves or renames the event in place (same id, so the row glides) and a delete takes it away; once DONE it is still laid over for two minutes unless the mirror has caught up (the added event is there by account, title and time; a changed event no longer matches what MEKA saw), so nothing jumps back before the poll; undone, clashed, refused and failed edits leave the provider's copy. `CalendarEvent.pendingEditId` / `isProvisional` (never stored). A provisional event has no MEKA actions (no prep, reminders, Leave by, hiding — `EventActions` writes no marks for it — or Edit: "Wait until Google has it"), and its detail shows its add's line (`EditEventRules.note`, `EventDetailView.provisional`). **Facade:** every view reads the overlaid events (`currentEvents()`: Today's timeline, Up next, the Calendar agenda, the brief, shutdown, review, search, notices and leave-by alarms). **Fold / Mac:** the event detail leaves out its action row for a provisional event (the line "Adding “Dentist” to Google" says why); rows already glide and collapse by id (Fold `animateItem`, Mac the lists' animations).   - *Slice 2c-iii, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly):* **the clash chooser**. **Server:** a clash now hands back their place and notes too (`theirLocation`, `theirNotes`; "" for none), so a resend is checked against all of their version. **Core** (ADR-008 addendum, additive LWW: `resolved`, `resolvedAtMs`, `resends`): `CalendarEdits.keepMine(clashId)` writes a new edit whose base is their version and whose changes are only what still differs from it (a delete deletes their version, the guests guard still applies), with the five-second Undo; undoing it asks the clash again; when their copy already reads as Meka's nothing is sent ("Google already has your version") and the clash is put away; needs editing still allowed. `keepTheirs(clashId)` sends nothing.   - *Slice 2d-ii, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly):* **natural typing in Add event's title**. **Core** (core/domain/EventTyping.kt, non-AI, pure): `EventTypingRules.parse` reads a trailing run of when-words only (so "Monday's team lunch" keeps its words), each kind once: days (today · tonight, 19:00 unless timed · tomorrow/tmrw · Mon…Sun, the coming one with today included unless its time has passed · "next Fri" = Friday of next week · "this Fri" · 12 Oct · Oct 12th · the 12th of October · Fri 9 Oct · 12/10 day/month · 1/3/2027; a date gone this year is next year's), times (3pm · 3:30pm · 3.30pm · 15:00 · at/@ · noon/midday; a bare "3" or "v2.10" never), ranges (3-4pm · 3:30-5pm · 11-1pm · 15:00–16:30 · from 3 to 5pm · 10pm-1am across midnight) and lengths with a unit (for 2h · 90 min · 1h30 · an hour · half an hour; "Dinner for 2" stays a table for two), and all day; nothing is read when nothing would be left of the title, when all day and a time clash, or for a lone lower-case "sun"/"sat" ("Walk in the sun"). `AddEventForm.typeTitle` (Add only; editing keeps `withTitle`, which reads nothing): new words fill the day, start, length or all day (a time with no day that has passed today means tomorrow); the same words again (an edit earlier in the title) leave choices made since alone; words that stop reading leave the fields and the title whole. - [x] Mac database encryption (spike S6); fix the Mac keychain prompts (no Apple-signed cert: use Secure Enclave-wrapped key files)
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-07 (Mac checked nightly):* the keychain prompts. `SealedKeyFiles` (Mac): the device id, household, enrolment secret, server address and the signing key's enclave handle now live in small files in Application Support/os.meka.mac/keys (folder 0700, files 0600, excluded from Time Machine), each sealed with a P-256 key-agreement key held in the Secure Enclave (its blob only works on this Mac, usable only while it's unlocked): a fresh ephemeral key per write, HKDF-SHA256 bound to the file's name, AES-256-GCM, so a copied, edited or swapped file won't open. Files carry no code-signature ACL, so an ad-hoc build no longer asks for the login keychain password after each rebuild. The first launch moves the old `os.meka.mac.identity.v3` Keychain values across once (all or nothing: if any can't be read, e.g. the prompt was cancelled, nothing is moved and that launch keeps the Keychain, so the device's identity is never lost; old items are left untouched). A Mac without a Secure Enclave keeps the Keychain. Unit-tested (`SealedKeyFilesTests`, with a software key standing in for the enclave). Mac only: the Fold already keeps its keys in the Android Keystore.
  - *Slice 2a, 2026-10-07 (Mac checked nightly):* the SQLCipher linkage (spike S6). The Mac app links Zetetic's official `SQLCipher.swift` package, pinned at 4.19.0 (BSD-style licence, text in docs/third-party-notices.md; free, open source, no service), instead of the system `-lsqlite3`, so MekaKit's SQLiter driver runs on SQLCipher. Without a key SQLCipher reads and writes a plain database exactly like SQLite, so Meka's existing database is untouched and still unkeyed in this slice. `MacDatabase.cipherVersion/opens` and `MacCoreFactory.databaseEncryptionProbe` work on throwaway databases only; `MekaCoreBridgeTests.testDatabaseEngineIsSQLCipher` asserts `PRAGMA cipher_version` is 4.x, that a keyed file has no plain SQLite header, reopens with its key, and is refused with the wrong key or none.
  - *Nightly Mac run green 2026-10-07 (run 37686245413, at bb03541): `testDatabaseEngineIsSQLCipher` passed, so slice 2b can go ahead.* Every "Mac checked nightly" item up to that commit is now checked.
  - *Slice 2b, 2026-10-08 (core and Mac touched, so Mac checked nightly; the Swift was written without a Mac to compile on, so the nightly run is its first build):* **the Mac database is encrypted**. **Core** (core/domain/DatabaseProtection.kt, non-AI, pure): `DatabaseProtectionRules.fileOf` (missing · plain "SQLite format 3" header · sealed), `plan(file, key)` → `DatabasePlan(setAside, encryptFirst, keyed)` (a plain file is encrypted first; a sealed file with no key, or beside a key made just now, is renamed aside, never deleted; without a key nothing is encrypted), `setAsideName` ("meka-set-aside-20261008-1606.db"), `macLine` (encrypted · no Secure Enclave, FileVault only · not yet, tries again next launch), `PHONE_LINE`, `setAsideLine`; DatabaseProtectionTest. `MacCoreFactory.databaseOpensWithKey` opens a file exactly as the core will. **Mac:** `DeviceIdentity.databaseKey()` keeps a 256-bit key as a sealed file only (`database_key`, written and read back before use; no Keychain fallback); `DatabaseEncryption` (macos/MekaOS/DatabaseEncryption.swift, `import SQLCipher`): before the core opens `meka.db`, SQLCipher is checked on throwaway files (the S6 probe), then a plain file is copied with `sqlcipher_export` into `meka.db.encrypting` (user_version carried over), checked (sealed header, the same rows in every table, opens through the core's driver with the key), swapped in (the plain file waits as `meka.db.plain-old` with its WAL/SHM), opened again, and only then the plain file is removed; any failure keeps the plain file and the next launch tries again; a swap interrupted by a quit is put back. `CoreModel` passes the key with `encrypted: true`; a set-aside file shows its one-time line. Your data says how the database is protected (Mac: the line above; Fold: "encrypted (SQLCipher, AES-256) with a key Android's Keystore holds"). ADR-002 addendum. Tests: DatabaseEncryptionTests (a plain database with a task encrypted once with every row kept and unreadable without the key, opened again next launch and by the core; no key / no SQLCipher changes nothing; a sealed file whose key is gone set aside and still opening with its own key; an interrupted swap put back). CI green (run 37808166768; macOS job at the next nightly run; the core was touched, so the server redeploys).
  - *Check on the Mac:* after the next `tools/install-mac.sh`, open MEKA: Today should look as before (nothing lost), and Ask → More → Your data should say "MEKA's database on this Mac is encrypted (SQLCipher, AES-256)…". If it says "isn't encrypted yet", the copy didn't check out and MEKA keeps the plain file; the next run looks at it.
  - *Check on the Mac:* after the next `tools/install-mac.sh`, macOS may ask for the login keychain password one last time (click Always Allow); after that, rebuilds shouldn't ask. If it keeps asking, the Mac has no Secure Enclave or the enclave refused the key: say so and the next run looks at it.

- [~] Clean-ups: orphan KMS key, restrict enrolment token after enrolment, ADR-004/005 updates
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-07:* the deploy gap: the service stack records the commit it was deployed from (`DeployedCommit` output, `-c commit=`), and `deploy.yml` diffs against that instead of `HEAD~1`, so a server commit whose CI failed followed by an app-only fix still deploys (no recorded commit, not an ancestor, or stack unreadable → deploy; infra test added). The enrolment code is narrowed (backend `enrolWithCode`, tested in memory over HTTP and on Postgres): it never brings back a revoked device (403 `revoked`; only the server's own `enrol-device` command can) and can't start a second household (403 `household`); a device that isn't revoked can still reconnect. Both apps show the refusal in words through the core (`Enrolment.refusal`, unit-tested; the Fold and Mac already display `Failed.reason`). ADR-004 and ADR-005 amended (P-256 device keys as built, sealed key files on the Mac, the enrolment rules, the deploy rule, the leftover KMS key). CI green (run 37577176613, after a test-only fix: wire ids are `[a-z0-9]`); deployed, so the stack now records its `DeployedCommit`.
  - *Remaining:* the orphan KMS key is Meka's to remove (Needs Meka #11; build runs never delete keys). Enrolling a brand-new device id is still open to whoever holds the code: that closes with passkey enrolment or "approve this new device on your Fold" (V1, with the Recovery key item); until then rotating the code after setting up a device shuts it.
  - *Seen 2026-10-06 (run 55):* `PostgresOpStoreTest.concurrentPushesGetGapFreeSequencesAndNoDuplicates` failed once with a Hikari connection timeout (`SQLTransientConnectionException` at the retry loop; Postgres logged "unexpected EOF on client connection with an open transaction") and passed on re-run; the commit didn't touch the backend. If it recurs, look for a connection held across the concurrent duplicate pushes in `PostgresOpStore` (pool of 8, 400 concurrent pushes).
  - *Fixed 2026-10-07 (recurred in run 37659606955):* `PostgresOpStore.conn` was `current.get()?.let(block) ?: ds.connection.use(block)`, so a block returning null (finding an op not stored yet) ran again on a second pooled connection while the push's transaction held its own; eight concurrent pushes then starved the pool of eight until Hikari's 30 s timeout. Now it uses the transaction's connection or a fresh one, never both (also halves the queries for every new op the server stores).

### V1 · The assistant layer
- [~] MEKA as the phone's default assistant (side-button long-press opens Ask) + voice: "move my dentist to Friday", "what did the school say?", "remind me to call Mum when I leave work".
  - *Talk to MEKA — conversation mode (Meka, 2026-10-08 12:55; take as the first V1 item once the AI layer's first slice lands):* a back-and-forth spoken conversation, not one-shot commands. Listen: on-device speech recognition (Android `SpeechRecognizer` offline English; Mac `SFSpeechRecognizer` on-device), nothing recorded or stored as audio. Understand and act: the AI layer with MEKA's own tools only (create/move/complete tasks, add/move calendar events, read Today/Needs you/brief/news/fasting, start a fast, set an alarm/timer) — every change goes through the policy engine at the same levels as tapping; anything that sends a message or email is drafted and needs a spoken or tapped "send it"; destructive changes ("delete…") confirm first. Speak: on-device text-to-speech (Android `TextToSpeech` with the best installed Google voice; Mac `AVSpeechSynthesizer` premium voices), short answers ("Added 'Call the dentist' for tomorrow at 9. Anything else?"), with an undo chip on screen for every change. Turn-taking: keeps listening after it answers until silence/“that's all”; barge-in (talking over it stops it). Starts from: a mic button in Ask and on Today's capture bar, the side-button long-press (default assistant), a home-screen widget, the bedside clock, and Bluetooth headset button; the Mac with ⌥Space. Read-outs: "read my brief", "what's the Barça news?", "read the headlines" (headlines read as plain text, untrusted — never acted on). No always-on wake word in the first version (battery, false triggers); revisit later. Motion: a voice orb that breathes while idle, swells with your voice level while listening, ripples while MEKA speaks, and the live transcript types in under it; reduced motion: a static ring with a level bar. Cost: speech in/out is free on device; each turn is one AI call (pennies), inside the $40 cap; show a daily count in Activity. A premium neural voice is a later option (needs Meka's OK on cost).
  - *Slice 1, 2026-10-08 (core touched, so Mac checked nightly; no app screen yet):* **the conversation**, core and server. **Core** (core/domain/Talk.kt, non-AI, pure): `TalkRules.reply` reads a final transcript: a goodbye ("that's all", "no thanks, bye", "ok cheers"; "ok" or "great" alone isn't one) ends; with cards on offer a yes ("yes please", "go ahead", "ok") confirms the one card, "the second one" / "the last one" / "the first and the third" / "both" (two only) / "all of them" pick, a bare yes with several makes MEKA ask which, a no ("no, leave it", "not now") leaves them on screen; anything else is a new question. What MEKA says: `spoken` (the answer made speakable: no list marks, markdown, quotes or emoji, "09:00–09:15" → "09:00 to 09:15", at most three sentences / 320 characters; then "Shall I move Book dentist to tomorrow at 09:00?" or "I can set a timer for 20 minutes or start a 36-hour fast. Say which, or both."), `spokenDone` ("Moved Book dentist to tomorrow at 09:00. Anything else?"), `spokenWhich`, `LEFT_IT`. `TalkFlow` is the turn-taking the apps will run: listen → ask → speak → listen again; silence or a goodbye ends quietly; talking over MEKA stops it and listens (barge-in); AI unavailable is said once and ends; twelve questions per conversation, then "That's a lot for one go. Tap the mic to carry on."; late events after a stop change nothing. `Conversation` keeps the exchanges in memory only (the last six are sent, with up to three confirmed lines each). **Wire:** `AskCodec.Request.history` (`[{q, a, done?}]`, at most six; limits refused) and `voice`, both left out of the JSON for a one-off question so older servers see the same request. **Server** (AiAsk.kt): history becomes alternating user/assistant turns (earlier answers as words only, so a card is only ever offered from the latest answer), what Meka confirmed leads the next question ("Since then Meka did: …"), the `<today>` block only in the latest turn; `voice` adds a line asking for one or two short sentences that read well aloud. **Facade:** `MekaCore.talk(question, history)` (one call, never throws, cards checked and policy-filtered as in `askMeka`); a spoken yes runs the cards through `doAsk` like a tap, so each change keeps its undo chip. ADR-006 addendum. Tests: TalkTest (goodbyes, choices, speakable lines, the flow incl. barge-in, which/no, unavailable, the cap, history), AskCodecTest (history round trip, limits, a one-off unchanged), AiAskTest (alternating turns, the day once, the voice line), AskMekaFacadeTest (a spoken conversation end to end: history sent, the yes moves the task, undo, goodbye). CI green (run 37825331281; macOS job at the next nightly run; the core was touched, so the server redeploys).
  - *Slice 2, 2026-10-08 (core touched, so Mac checked nightly; the Fold app only):* **talking on the Fold**. **Core** (Talk.kt, non-AI, pure): `TalkVoice.best` picks the voice (installed, on the device only, never a network voice; English, British first, then quality, then name); `TalkOrb` gives the orb's numbers for both apps (`level` from the recogniser's dB, `smooth` up fast/down slow, `scale` swelling 22 % with the voice or breathing, three `ripple`s per 1.4 s while speaking, the `label`); `TalkProblem` (no microphone permission · no on-device recogniser, so it won't listen and nothing is sent away · English being fetched · busy · couldn't hear) said on screen; `TalkDid` and `MekaCore.doTalk(cards)` (a spoken yes does each card exactly as a tap; one that can't be done any more is counted, the rest still go) with `undoTalk` (one undo bar takes them all back, newest first). **Fold** (ask/TalkController.kt, ask/VoiceOrb.kt, AskMekaSection): a mic beside Ask's field asks for RECORD_AUDIO the first time and starts a conversation run by the core's `TalkFlow`: Android's **on-device** `SpeechRecognizer` only (en-GB, partial results typing in; if the phone has none MEKA says so instead of using a cloud recogniser; a missing language pack is fetched on Android 13+), `TextToSpeech` with the chosen voice (assistant audio), answers shown in Ask as typed ones (cards still tappable; a card tapped by hand is no longer on offer aloud) and said aloud, a spoken yes folds the cards away with one undo bar; tapping the orb while MEKA speaks interrupts it (barge-in by touch, since the recogniser would hear MEKA through the speaker), otherwise ends; leaving Ask or the app stops it. Manifest: RECORD_AUDIO and package-visibility queries for the recogniser and the speech engine. Motion catalogue row "Talk to MEKA (voice orb)". ADR-006 addendum. Tests: TalkTest (voice choice, orb numbers, one undo bar, the problem lines), AskMekaFacadeTest (`doTalk` does what it can and one undo takes them back). If it says the phone can't recognise speech on the device: install offline English for speech recognition on the phone (Google's speech services, "Offline speech recognition" → English (UK)), then try again; say what you see and the next run looks at it.

The orb is the Fold's, number for number (`VoiceOrbView`: breath, swell to 1.22× with a brass fill, three 1.4 s ripples; reduced motion: still ring and level bar); the panel unfolds with the expand spring. Info.plist `NSMicrophoneUsageDescription` / `NSSpeechRecognitionUsageDescription`; entitlement `com.apple.security.device.audio-input` (hardened runtime). ADR-006 addendum. Concurrency: the mic tap is set up in a nonisolated static (its closure runs on the audio thread and touches only the request and a lock); recogniser and speech callbacks hop back with Strings/Bools/Ints; the question/history and the cards each cross into the core once.
